# 101 — The round's open gaps: one ledger, each with an owner (2026-10-01)

*(Web sprint 6, MATCH-03 ([`../roadmap/07-match.md`](../roadmap/07-match.md)). A ledger, not a reading: every known
deviation of today's round from the original, listed once, each with the line it comes from, how it would be validated
and who owns it. Swept 2026-10-01 on `agent/web-s6-match` (the integration branch at a0267a76 plus MATCH-04's audit,
cd380d12). No game or emulator was run.)*

## 0. The answer

MLS reconciliation, 2026-10-23: §5's live table has 417 rows after retiring the ARMORY mode name and listing the
native Options serializer's existing card password exclusion. ONL-13's live table has three rows. The global figures
below retain their dated recount; this scoped correction is not a fresh global population count.

**1,221 rows: 794 in §2 and §4 -- §2's 44 gaps and §4's 750 named stand-ins, 23 of them marked settled -- plus §3's 12
rulings and §5's 415 names excluded and why; none of the sources' items left without a row.** *(Recounted from the tables 2026-10-22, research 347 §1, which
files the 794 by what the game's decrypted code would settle; was: "433 rows: 278 open, 227 of them the round's named
stand-ins (one row a constant), plus 155 names excluded and why", the count of 2026-10-05 below.)* *(Recounted from the rows themselves 2026-10-05, sprint 17's close
review, pass 2 -- §2's 41 rows (A7 is its constant's), §3's 10, §4's 227 and §5's 155 (154 at pass 2, pass 3's `SWITCH_CLOSED_AT_COUNTDOWN_READING` after), by each row's first owner and its
kind cell; `RELOAD_BLEND_PLACEHOLDER`, resolved 2026-10-03, moved from §4 to §5 then. The headline had read 383 /
276 / 225 / 107 and the owners platform 48, online 40, options 5 while the merges of 2026-10-05 added rows below it.
Before that, recounted from the tables 2026-10-05, PR #10's fix round: the
count read 312 / 264 / 213 / 48 from 2026-10-01 while the later merges added rows below it; PF-05, 2026-10-04: +2 open (PLAT-36), +1 (OC-M3), +3 excluded; the R2 menu's draw, 2026-10-05 (research 212): three OC-M3 rows to §5, three added (`INVENTORY_CHANGE_FLASH_READING` its own row in the review round) -- +3 excluded. ONL-18b, 2026-10-06
(research 221): `LOAD_BARRIER_TIMEOUT_PLACEHOLDER` added to §4 (ONL-18's, the page's own), `SLOW_LOADER_PLACEHOLDER`
and `JOIN_DECIDED_PHASE_READING` retired to §5 -- the figures here not recounted for it.)* *[2026-10-15, sprint 40 AD-0 (the

not today's count of unresolved deviations; a row's current state is its own task and the Log that records it,
which outrank a stale "open" in a table here or a "pending review" banner. The reconciliation (its chunk 02)
routed a finite set to its 95-row ledger and substituted no global recount.]* By kind:

**OC-M3 is the one finding the ledger was for.** Segment 07 leaves the round itself to web sprints 1 to 5 and their
plans ([`07-match.md`](../roadmap/07-match.md), its introduction), and those plans are closed: 136 rows -- 109 named
readings and placeholders and 27 unnamed gaps (A7 moved to its constant, 2026-10-02) -- have no task in the roadmap. Most need one of two instruments the
roadmap already has: a recomp trace (PLAT-36's window) or a console capture (PLAT-31's manifest, scored by PLAT-30);
some need only a decomp read. Placing them (a MATCH task per area, or extending PLAT-36's list) is the owner's call,
recorded here as **OC-M3** and not ruled.

**How the rows were found.** The sources MATCH-03's block names, each swept whole: [`../../README.md`](../../README.md)
"Known gaps" (L800-850) and its controls (L220-551); web research 90 (all of it: §2's and §6-§8's issue tables, §8.1's
re-play, §9.3-§9.4); every `*_PLACEHOLDER` and `*_READING` named in the placeholder sections of web research 72-95
(246 names, the walk of `tools/test/placeholderLedger.test.ts`: 190 held by the round's sources -- `viewer` 171,
`server` 30, `scene` 12, `sound` 2, some in more than one -- and 56 named only in a note; today, 2026-10-05, the round's sources hold 262 -- `viewer` 217, `server` 54, `scene` 12,
`sound` 10); the PC-over-console rulings
[`../MISSION.md`](../MISSION.md) lists (L135-141); the sprint 5 plan's Log
([`../plans/2026-09-30-web-sprint-5-armory.md`](../plans/2026-09-30-web-sprint-5-armory.md)); the local handoff's §5
and §6 ([`../plans/2026-09-29-local-handoff.md`](../plans/2026-09-29-local-handoff.md) L229-246); research 84 §7.1
(MATCH-04's audit, today); and what ONL-19, ONL-20, ONL-21, ONL-22, MATCH-01 and MATCH-02 already own, cited, not
restated. A source's item that a later document closed is listed in §5 with what closed it, not as a row.

**The grouping.** A row per constant for the named stand-ins (§4), grouped by owner, because each is a separate value
standing in for the original's; one row per item for the gaps no constant names (§2); one row per ruling (§3). An
item that is the same deviation as a constant (the README's "hand-off phases", the handoff's "Triangle hold time") is
the constant's row and is cited there, not repeated. `tools/test/roundLedger.test.ts` keeps §4 and §5 whole: every
`*_PLACEHOLDER` / `*_READING` the round's sources hold is the first cell of a row of one or the other.

**The columns.** *Listed at*: the web research note and line where the name is listed (`R84 L533` is research 84's
line 533), or the source line of an unnamed item. *Validation*: what would close it -- "owed: capture" (a console or
recomp frame, PLAT-31's manifest, which does not exist yet), "owed: trace" (PLAT-36's window), "owed: read" (a decomp
read), "owed: measure (W2.2c)" (the console speed measurement of research 79), "PLAT-33's method" (the audio
goldens), "PLAT-30" (the capture scorer), "PLAT-32's trace" (the UI script goldens), or a test id where one holds the
value today. No row is validated by a capture yet: PLAT-31's manifest is not built.

## 1. Where the round stands

The round runs from the loading screen (ONL-18) to the round and final screens (ONL-20): the SEAL walking and fighting
(sprints 1-2), the match server and its rules (sprint 3), the arsenal (sprint 4), the ARMORY and the character types
(sprint 5), first person and the binoculars (2026-10-01, research 84 §7, §7.1). Its fidelity is at the **reading**
level almost everywhere: read from the decompilation, held by unit tests, not yet measured against the original
running (MISSION.md, "Validation is our confidence measure"). The rows below are what is known to differ or to be
unread; a gap nobody has noticed is not here, which is why the validation column matters more than the count.

| A11 | the two facade modes: **read** (#291, 2026-10-18; FACADES-2 the same day; reading, pending review). `FUN_00317fc0` (D:L216502-216595) replaces the rotation rows of the node's world matrix and keeps its origin: the rows start from the identity (`FUN_001bff28`, D:L216540) and are written whole (D:L216584-216595), so the node's own and its parents' rotation **and scale** are dropped while it is turned. Facade 1 turns about the vertical; facade 2 then tilts, its third row the unit vector from the camera to the node (`FUN_00309200` D:L216541, `FUN_001bfc30` D:L216569); a camera straight above collapses it. The visual keeps its cull (`FUN_003b5f20` D:L307850), so a culled quad wound to face +z is not seen from beyond its own reach: MP6's checker cross under the garage's light and MP9's 49 far-tree markers (from 120 units, 22 of the 49 show a corner, the original's too by the reading). The draw walk turns every node so flagged, world model or prop (`FUN_003389c0` D:L236080-236081): since FACADES-2 the world model's own facade nodes are turned too, 23 on 7 maps (MP7's moon and five stars, MP52's four pipes, MP11's four glows, MP53's and MP71's suns, M72's six smoke columns, M82's `g4445`), drawn as facade props (`./loadMap` `place`); M82's `g4445` (four culled `flare_red.tif` triangles wound to face +z, facade 2) is now hidden from beyond its reach, as the original hides it by the reading, where the port drew it fixed before. **The alpha pass's key:** the engine keys an alpha-pass visual by the range from the camera to its centroid (`vparams` +0xc) carried through the matrix it is drawn with, copied into the list entry (`FUN_003371b0` D:L235289, D:L235314-235317) -- for a facade the turned one, so the key moves as it turns and is measured anew each frame; with the engine order on (`setEngineOrder`) the port keys a turned facade so, each frame (`./facade` `facadeAlphaKey`). In the default order three sorts the pass by the clip-space depth of the geometry's bounding-sphere centre through the turned `matrixWorld`, its view depth to the turned box centre: not the original's key (FACADES-2's census: 53 of 48,479 overlapping facade camera-pairs ordered otherwise, all on MP9's far-tree cards; `./world` `restOrder`). **The lighting:** the engine lights a node through the matrix it draws it with -- `FUN_003b6d10` (called at D:L235428 in the walk, D:L235165 in the alpha pass) keeps it and its inverse (D:L308338-308339) and `FUN_003b6870` carries the light block through them (D:L308207-308208) -- so a lit facade is lit under its turned normals; the port re-lights a lit facade part each frame it turns (`./facade` `relightFacade`, `FacadeRelight`): MP61's 32 lit glows (24 `xmaslight/flare` and 8 `light_round_nosource` that `CLUTTER.ZAR` places) and M51's 167 lit clutter tree cards (`oaktree_facade*`, `thintree_facade*`). The re-light is guarded on cost alone, with no visible change: a part is lit again only when its turn or the lighting it reads (the rig, `rigEverywhere`) changed, or the pose on disc was put back or the turn resumed. A prop's texture scroll is kept as a world part's (M72's smoke columns, and every prop's: 180 parts on 8 maps). `./facade`, wired in `./world` and `./loadMap`; pinned by `packages/viewer/test/facade.test.ts`, `worldFacade.test.ts` and `worldFacadeDisc.test.ts` (the census in `facade.test.ts` is the scene graph's, not `CLUTTER.ZAR`'s). 10 of the 395 facade placements carry a scale, and the original and the port both draw all 10 at 1x while turned (the turn writes unit rows). Against their placed size, 5 differ by more than this note's own 0.67-1.5x reporting threshold (a judgement, not the original's): M63's three `lpole01_15..17` flares (placed at 0.5, drawn at 1: twice the size) and M82's two `danger_light_1/2` flares (0.6, drawn at 1); MP83's five `light_wall02` flares (1.5, drawn at 1) are at the threshold. **The matrix stack (LATER 120, 2026-10-19; reading, pending review):** the walk pushes an entry only for a node whose local matrix is not identity-like (`FUN_003084f0` D:L206890-206916: the translation words exactly 0 and the diagonal words exactly 1.0, compared as bit patterns, the off-diagonal words not read; D:L236075-236079); the turn rewrites the entry on top (D:L236081) -- the node's own, or the one an identity-like node shares -- and only a node that pushed pops (D:L236222-236225); the port takes each world child as an atom drawn on a fresh entry (`FUN_00337f40` D:L235715-235717, popped D:L235753-235754; a reading on an unread bit, below); children are drawn in file order (`FUN_00315f40` appends, D:L215358-215359 -- reCOM's `CNode::AddChild` inserts at the front, `node_main.cpp:425`; the decomp decides). So the nodes drawn after an identity-like facade node on the entry it shares turn with it: on the 34 maps only MP11's 13 `light_bulb` assemblies, whose `flare` is drawn first and turns the instance's entry, so `bulb_1`, `wire` (a GS line strip) and `frame` turn whole with it, their placed rotation dropped (39 placements, facade 0 -> 2; the scene-graph golden's MP11 `facades` 18 -> 57). The port: `flattenScene`'s `facade` is the turn a node is drawn under (`identityLike`, `StackEntry`); a turned placement's strips are a line group of their own in model space, turned with it (`LoadedLineGroup.turn`); a facade value of 3 writes no turn (`FUN_00317fc0` returns first; none on the disc), where the port had marked the node 3. The sweep (2026-10-19): no other facade placement of the 34 maps, scene graph or `CLUTTER.ZAR`, draws a line strip. Pinned by `packages/scene/test/facadeStack.test.ts` and `packages/viewer/test/worldFacade.test.ts`, `worldFacadeDisc.test.ts` and `facade.test.ts`. | A43 | after a session master's ABORT the other page left the round too: **The port today**, by evidence kind: *seen* (rendered browser, MATCH-02's run, 20261013T0458Z / 0501Z, two pages): the session master's ABORT took the other page out of the round, and the other page's frame shows SOCOM II ONLINE; *read, not seen* (source inspection): the path between. The leaver's socket closes and the match room's `leave` runs; `room.ts` `abandonIfSideEmpty` / `abandon` only puts the room back into waiting (`abandoned`). What ends the game is the bridge's `ABANDON_BELOW_PLAYERS_READING` (3) rule, on every leave of a launched game, a side emptied or not (`bridge.ts` `leave`, :480-494, and `onAbandon`, :617-624; the constant :757): fewer than three players left, `endGame`, so the other page's `ended` is #528 and `lobbyMatch.ts` `returnDialog` sends it to `dlgNetAbandoned.rdr`, whose script returns to SOCOM II ONLINE. `lobby.ts` `hostGone` is for unlaunched games only (`bridge.ts` :484-488). `dlgNetAbandoned.rdr` in between is read from `returnDialog`, not seen in the run. The original: **The original, as far as read** (MATCH-02b): each other console's ClientLeaves `FUN_002bc530` (D:L161111-161146) clears the leaver's slot and counts the slots left (D:L161124-161132); then, by the game object's +0xcc, either `FUN_002c00f0` (+0xcc clear, D:L161134-161135: a record refresh that runs only for the session master, its guard `DAT_0045a0c1` and `DAT_0045a0c0` both set, D:L163049) or, +0xcc set, `dlgNetAbandoned.rdr` when `FUN_0030d510(0x45a0c0)` less the count is under 3 (`FUN_002a47b0(0x4364e0, S:0x3f2780)`, D:L161137-161141). **What +0xcc means is not read, and two readings conflict**: research 187 §7.4 (+0xcc's meaning BELIEVED there; #528 reads "All players left the game before you were able to join.") and MATCH-02 took +0xcc set as a console still joining (the late joiner), so in a round only the refresh runs; the server takes the +0xcc-set branch as the in-round abandon below three (`bridge.ts` :619-621, :749; `room.ts` :1915-1918, citing D:L161130-161140). +0xcc's other uses do not settle it: D:L55862 (multiplayer, not the session master, +0xcc set: `FUN_002a7490(game, 2)`), D:L125603-125609 (a timed branch: clear `FUN_002c3fc0(game, 2)`, set `FUN_002a7420` and `FUN_002a73b0(game, 1)`), D:L160945 (a state-4 message, +0xcc set: `FUN_002a7340(game, 0)`); none of the four callees is read. *2026-10-14 (WATCH-DOCS; research 315 §3.3, R315.4): **+0xcc is the late flag** -- `FUN_002a7340` (D:L149273-149280) stores it together with the valve `late_joiner` (the game record's +0x2c, S:0x3f10f8, D:L149565-149566), and the load state clears it once loaded (D:L55711-55716; research 314 §4.2); of the uses above, D:L125603-125609 is `ReadyToLoad`'s late branch (`FUN_0027caf0`: `FUN_002a7420` writes the major state 2, `FUN_002a73b0` the minor 1; W19.R12, 314 §4.2) and D:L160945 clears the flag on the network's message 4 (`FUN_002bbfa0` D:L160943-160947). So the conflict leans to research 187's and MATCH-02's reading -- the below-three `dlgNetAbandoned.rdr` is a late joiner's, and in a round a leave that leaves two players shows no #528 on the seated consoles; the server is not changed on that alone (`ABANDON_LATE_JOINER_READING`, note only in research 315 §7; W19.R10): what a round does with a side emptied or two players left is unread, and ONL-32's capture B settles both. Reading, pending review.* | R266 §8.10.5, §8.11.3, R315 §3.3 | gap (a conflict of readings, named) | owed: ONL-32's two-console capture: one console aborts in a round, the other's screen recorded to its end (the refresh only, or `dlgNetAbandoned.rdr`), which settles +0xcc and the rule together | MATCH-02 |

The demonstrated headless cause of the live refusals is `aimBodies`' shape (28 of 127); the reconstructed-eye divergence is the moving/turning hypothesis, held by the root-divergence case and closed by construction. At contacts inside the cut no offer changes and the target differs by up to 8.21 in height (416 over 2) and 5.52 in xz. Those edge contacts exist partly because the port's walk hit is the nearest point clamped to the cut's end, where the original's hits are segment crossings that reach that column at almost no point (`CLIMB_COLUMN_END_READING`); the 23.64 comes from that reading together with the frame. *Corrected by LATER 142 (2026-10-19, R313 §8.10):* those offer flips were not the frame but f32 rounding at the cut's end vertex -- the walk's hit on the polygon's boundary, where the column is ill-conditioned in either frame: at the first pinned spot a hit moved 1e-5 inward is offered in the collider's frame, and at the second the collider's 34.25 becomes the world frame's 57.88 1e-4 inward; at the hit taken 0.01 inside its cut (`CLIMB_EDGE_INSET_PLACEHOLDER`) the frame changes 14 offers of 100,554 static-body contacts on the fences (fence02's two top corners, offered in the collider's frame only, at a still body's contact selection; through the port's walk neither is a refusal, LATER 142's re-review G1) and the target's height up to 9.62 (2,519 over 2). **Closed since their source, so not rows.** Research 90: items 1, 4, 5 on metal, 7-10, 12-16 (§8.1), 18 and 19 (§9.3), 2
and 6 (closed as the game's), 11 (the jump-grab: research 86); from §9.4, #21 (the WebGL2 walk-entry stall, fixed by a
rehearsal draw), #22 (prone refused at three spawns: the game's water rule, research 86 §6.4), #23 (the WebGL2 blast
hitch), #24 (the goggles' sounds: HUDUI loaded with every map), #26 (Death Trap's default material fixed; Vigilance's
`water_drain` is the game's own, research 81 L393-398), #27's cause (the context made at page start; its re-measure is
A19), the `3` key, the rifle scope's sides at 16:9, the faint bullet marks (research 89 §13), the WebGL2 runs owed on
seven maps (all in the local handoff, L105-127); §8's row 5 (the bounce silent on asphalt) is A15's mend. The sprint 5
Log's two items found open (2026-10-01 ~08:30Z: a closed browser's seat held 5-10 s; a frag thrown before round 1
going off in it) were both closed at 11:56Z the same day. The handoff's §5: item 2 is B1, item 3's claymore online
was built (web sprint 4 M7, `CLAYMORE_PLACEHOLDER` retired), the radio menu's look is `RADIO_MENU_PLACEHOLDER`, the
spectator views are ONL-21. Research 95 §8's last row (L1071) still calls the binoculars "an open owner question": the
owner answered it the same day (B3); that row is stale, not open.

**What the ledger does not cover, and why.**
- **The front end** (boot, the menus, the options, the memory card, the online screens up to the loading screen):
  segments 01-06 own every one of their gaps task by task; this ledger is the round's (MATCH-03's goal).
- **The site and the page's chrome**: the favicon's 404 (research 90 #25), the developer hooks left ungated (the
  handoff's §5 item 5), the release gate's own owed items (§5 item 1). None is a deviation of the round.
- **The auto-exposure slider** (README L838-840): a multiplayer round never runs the game's metering, so the round does
  not differ; the campaign is out of scope (MISSION.md).
- **The ISO reader's reach** (README L841-850): one retail disc proven; a property of the loader, not of the round.
- **The slot outlines drawn without a depth test** (README L820-822): a developer overlay over the collision slots,
  not drawn in a round.
- **Six packages**, which `tools/test/roundLedger.test.ts` does not walk (it walks only the round's four: `viewer`,
  `server`, `scene`, `sound`): `ui`, `card`, `fmv` and `archive` are the front end's packages (and the disc reader's),
  not the round's; `gs` and `mesh` are the decoders. None of the six holds a `*_PLACEHOLDER` / `*_READING` name today;
  a name one of them gains belongs to its own task's note.
- **Gaps nobody has noticed.** A ledger lists what is known. The round's fidelity is at the reading level until
  PLAT-31's captures and PLAT-36's traces exist; the validation column says, row by row, what would find the rest.

## 7. The two matches' equivalence, audited 2026-10-13

*(SVC-25, sprint 18: the owner's word of 2026-10-13, "ensuring all our recent game and sound work exists on match servers
and not just the test room"; OT.R43. Source inspection and unit tests only, on `agent/online` bd907029d; no browser, no
server, no game run.)* For each recent feature: its mount in `/testroom` (`packages/viewer/src/main.ts`), its mount in
the lobby-launched match (`packages/viewer/src/matchMount.ts`, mounted by `frontEnd.ts:828`), its path on the room a
lobby-created game gets (`bridge.ts:572` `open` -> `server.ts:680` `makeRoom`, stepped from `addRoom`), and the test
that holds it. Line numbers are at bd907029d.

**The one check for the gameplay rows:** a game's room is `new Room(...)` (`server.ts:689`), the class a map's room is
(`server.ts:643`), on the server's own room options under the game's (`server.ts:686`); a seat's binary frames go to
`session.room.binary` (`server.ts:860`) and its text, past the game lobby's own four (`bridge.ts:463-470`), to
`session.room.text` (`server.ts:927-929`) for both kinds. So every rule `room.ts` holds is the game's too. On the page
the parts are switched by `matchCore.ts`'s `TESTROOM_CAPABILITIES` and `FRONT_END_CAPABILITIES`, pinned by
`matchCore.test.ts`: they differ only in `tacMap` and `replay` (below).

**Gaps found: none in the code.** Three holds were missing and are added with this audit: a lobby-created game's room
relaying voice and taunts, counted under its own name, and refusing an unplayed map (`packages/central/test/gameRoomParts.test.ts`),
and the lobby match's round drain (`packages/viewer/test/matchMount.test.ts`). The tactical map is not a gap: the
original's multiplayer round has none (above). Not game behaviour, and older than this sprint (the review's F2): the
server's once-a-second `dropped` log, `/metrics`' per-room gauges and `/health`'s room count covered the map rooms only
(`server.ts:1209-1212`, `:1110`, `:1034` then) -- a lobby game's dropped rounds and throws were counted in its `Room.drops`
but never logged. Closed by LATER 41 (2026-10-21): the three cover the games' rooms by their names
(`packages/central/test/gameRoomInstruments.test.ts`).
